<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the higher-dimensional Thomason model-structure conjecture of Ara and Maltsiniotis. For every $`1\le n\le\infty`$, the category of small strict globular <i>n</i>-categories admits a proper combinatorial model structure that is Quillen equivalent to simplicial sets. Its weak equivalences and fibrations are detected by the twice-extended Street nerve $`\mathrm{Ex}^2N_n`$, and the Quillen equivalence is given by $`c_n\mathrm{Sd}^2\dashv\mathrm{Ex}^2N_n`$. Thus strict higher categories model the homotopy theory of spaces in every positive finite dimension and in dimension <i>ω</i>.

- **claim-thomason-model-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every $n\in\{1,2,3,\ldots\}\cup\{\infty\}$, the classes $(C_n,W_n,F_n)$ in <a href="#eq:prescribed-classes" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prescribed-classes">[eq:prescribed-classes]</a> form a proper combinatorial model structure on $n\mathrm{Cat}$. It is cofibrantly generated by $(L_nI,L_nJ)$, where $$I=\{\partial\Delta[m]\hookrightarrow\Delta[m]:m\ge0\},\qquad J=\{\Lambda^k[m]\hookrightarrow\Delta[m]:m\ge1,\ 0\le k\le m\}.$$ Moreover, $$L_n:\mathrm{sSet}_{\mathrm{KQ}}\rightleftarrows(n\mathrm{Cat},C_n,W_n,F_n):R_n$$ is a Quillen equivalence.
  - Explanation: For every positive finite dimension and for the infinite case, the stated classes make strict globular n-categories a proper combinatorial model structure generated by the transferred boundary and horn inclusions, Quillen equivalent to simplicial sets.
- **claim-internal-hom-truncation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-internal-truncation)): Let $n<\infty$ and let $Y$ be a strict $n$-category. Then $H(Y)$ is a strict $n$-category. Consequently the reflection unit $X\to\tau_nX$ induces a natural bijection $$\label{eq:truncated-cylinder-bijection} \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\omega\mathrm{Cat}}((\tau_nX)\otimes D_{1},Y) \ \xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\ \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\omega\mathrm{Cat}}(X\otimes D_{1},Y).$$ This bijection respects endpoints and restriction along functors in the first variable. For $n=\infty$ the same assertion holds with $\tau_n=\mathrm{id}$.
  - Explanation: The right internal hom of an n-category is again an n-category, so cylinders out of a tensor with the interval factor through the reflection.
- **claim-gray-cylinder-nerve-homotopy** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-gray-nerve)): There is a natural simplicial map $$J_X:N_\infty X\times\Delta[1]\longrightarrow N_\infty(X\otimes D_{1})$$ whose restriction at the endpoint $a$ is $N_\infty(\mathrm{id}_X\otimes e_a)$, and for which $N_\infty(\pi_X)J_X$ is the projection to $N_\infty X$. Consequently a strict functor $P:X\otimes D_{1}\to Y$ induces a simplicial homotopy between the nerves of its endpoint functors. If a functor $a:A\to X$ satisfies $P(a\otimes\mathrm{id})=h\pi_A$ for some $h:A\to Y$, the homotopy restricts along $N_\infty a$ to the constant homotopy of $N_\infty h$. The same assertions apply to $N_n$ when $X,Y$ are $n$-categories.
  - Explanation: A right Gray cylinder yields a natural simplicial homotopy between the nerves of its endpoint functors, stationary where the cylinder is.
- **claim-filtered-colimits-presentability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-filtered)): For every $1\le n\le\infty$, the category $n\mathrm{Cat}$ is locally finitely presentable, and filtered colimits are computed on cells. Each $\tau_n\mathcal O_{m}$ is finitely presentable in $n\mathrm{Cat}$. Consequently $N_n$, and also $\mathrm{Ex}^rN_n$ for every finite $r\ge0$, preserve filtered colimits. Simplicial weak equivalences are closed under filtered colimits of arrows, as are the maps $f$ of $n\mathrm{Cat}$ for which $N_nf$ is a weak equivalence. No monomorphism hypothesis on transition maps is required.
  - Explanation: Strict n-categories are locally finitely presentable with cellwise filtered colimits, so the nerve and its Ex powers preserve filtered colimits and weak equivalences are closed under them.
- **claim-simplicial-gluing-one-mono** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-simplicial-gluing)): Consider a commutative map of spans of simplicial sets $$\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large] A \arrow[d,"a"'] & C \arrow[l] \arrow[r,hook,"i"] \arrow[d,"c"'] & B \arrow[d,"b"]\\ A' & C' \arrow[l] \arrow[r,hook,"i'"'] & B'. \end{tikzcd}$$ where $i$ and $i'$ are monomorphisms. If $a,b,c$ are weak equivalences, then the canonical map $$A\amalg_C B\longrightarrow A'\amalg_{C'}B'$$ is a weak equivalence. There is no condition on the maps $C\to A$ and $C'\to A'$. Each of the displayed ordinary pushouts computes a homotopy pushout. In particular, cobase change of a weak equivalence along a simplicial monomorphism is a weak equivalence.
  - Explanation: Gluing of weak equivalences of spans holds when only one leg of each span is a monomorphism.
- **claim-relative-prism** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-relative-prism)): Let $A\subset E$ be a downward-closed subposet, and let $f,g:E\to E$ be monotone maps such that $$f(e)\le g(e)\quad(e\in E),\qquad f\|_A=g\|_A, \qquad g\|_{E\setminus A}\text{ is constant}.$$ The constancy condition is vacuous when $E\setminus A$ is empty. For each $1\le n\le\infty$ there is a strict functor of $\omega$-categories $$P_{f,g}:U_{E}\otimes D_{1}\longrightarrow U_{E}$$ whose restrictions to the two interval endpoints are $U(f)$ and $U(g)$. If $i:A\hookrightarrow E$ and $v=f i=g i:A\to E$, then $$\label{eq:prism-stationarity} P_{f,g}(U(i)\otimes\mathrm{id}_{D_{1}})=U(v)\pi_{U_{A}}.$$ Consequently its endpoints induce simplicially homotopic maps on Street nerves, with the homotopy stationary relative to $N_nU(v)$.
  - Explanation: Monotone maps ordered pointwise, agreeing on a sieve and constant off it, give a strict cylinder stationary on the old part.
- **claim-pushout-cylinder-gluing** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-pushout-cylinder)): Let $i:B_0\to B$ and $a:B_0\to X$ be arbitrary strict functors of $n$-categories, and form the pushout $$\begin{tikzcd} B_0\ar[r,"i"]\ar[d,"a"']&B\ar[d,"b"]\\ X\ar[r,"j"']&Y. \end{tikzcd}$$ Suppose $F_0,F_1:B\to B$ satisfy $F_0i=F_1i=i$, and suppose $P:B\otimes D_{1}\to B$ has endpoints $F_0,F_1$ and satisfies $P(i\otimes\mathrm{id})=i\pi_{B_0}$. Then there is a cylinder $Q:Y\otimes D_{1}\to Y$ stationary on $X$, whose endpoints $\overline F_0,\overline F_1:Y\to Y$ are specified by $$\overline F_\delta j=j,\qquad \overline F_\delta b=bF_\delta\qquad(\delta=0,1).$$ Its restriction along $b\otimes\mathrm{id}$ is $bP$. This construction is natural in $a:B_0\to X$, and its nerve gives a simplicial homotopy between $N_n\overline F_0$ and $N_n\overline F_1$ stationary along $N_nj$. In particular it applies to Lemma <a href="#lem:relative-prism" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:relative-prism">7</a> whenever $f\|_A=g\|_A=\mathrm{id}_A$, for every attaching functor $U_{A}\to X$.
  - Explanation: A cylinder stationary on the attaching source glues along any pushout of n-categories to a cylinder stationary on the target.
- **claim-fibers-preserve-colimits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fibers)): For $\epsilon\in\{0,1\}$, the functor $$F_\epsilon:n\mathrm{Cat}/D_{1}\longrightarrow n\mathrm{Cat}, \qquad (C\xrightarrow{p}D_{1})\longmapsto C_\epsilon=p^{-1}(\epsilon),$$ preserves all small colimits. The object functor $\operatorname{Ob}:n\mathrm{Cat}\to\mathrm{Set}$ also preserves all small colimits. Both assertions hold directly for $n=\infty$.
  - Explanation: Taking fibers over either endpoint of the one-cell category and taking object sets both preserve small colimits.
- **claim-oriental-segments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-oriental-segments)): Let $I$ be an initial or a final segment of $[m]=\{0,\ldots,m\}$. If $I$ is nonempty and has $q+1$ vertices, the face inclusion identifies $\tau_n\mathcal O_{q}$ with the full sub-$n$-category of $\tau_n\mathcal O_{m}$ on $I$. If $I$ is empty, that full subcategory is empty. Here and below $\tau_n=\mathrm{id}$ when $n=\infty$.
  - Explanation: An initial or final vertex segment of an oriental spans a full subcategory that is again an oriental.
- **claim-poset-pieces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-poset-pieces)): There is a canonical identification $\operatorname{Ob}U_{P}=P$ for every poset $P$. If $S\subset P$ is a sieve, the map $U_{S}\to U_{P}$ is the inclusion of the full $0$-fiber of the membership label $U_{P}\to D_{1}$. If $S$ is a cosieve, it is the inclusion of the full $1$-fiber. These identifications are natural for maps of labelled posets and include empty posets and empty fibers.
  - Explanation: Objects of the categorified poset are the poset, and sieves and cosieves are full fibers of the membership label.
- **claim-labelled-attachments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-labelled-pushout)): Let $A\subset P$ be a full subposet, let $a:U_{A}\to X$ be any strict functor, and form $$Y=X\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{P}.$$ Suppose a monotone map $\lambda:P\to[1]$ and a strict functor $\xi:X\to D_{1}$ satisfy $$\xi a=U(\lambda\|_A):U_{A}\longrightarrow D_{1}, \qquad U_{[1]}=D_{1}.$$ Write $P_\epsilon=\lambda^{-1}(\epsilon)$, $A_\epsilon=A\cap P_\epsilon$, and $X_\epsilon=\xi^{-1}(\epsilon)$. Then the induced label $Y\to D_{1}$ has actual full fibers $$\label{eq:pushout-fiber} Y_\epsilon\cong X_\epsilon\amalg_{U_{A_\epsilon}}U_{P_\epsilon}.$$ Moreover, independently of these labels, $$\label{eq:pushout-objects} \operatorname{Ob}Y\cong \operatorname{Ob}X\amalg_A P \cong\operatorname{Ob}X\amalg(P\setminus A).$$ For two such pairs of compatible labels, write $\bar\lambda_1,\bar\lambda_2:Y\to D_{1}$ for the induced labels. Their $0$-fibers intersect in the $0$-fiber of $\max\circ(\bar\lambda_1,\bar\lambda_2)$; their $1$-fibers intersect in the $1$-fiber of $\min\circ(\bar\lambda_1,\bar\lambda_2)$. Formula <a href="#eq:pushout-fiber" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pushout-fiber">[eq:pushout-fiber]</a>, using the corresponding maximum or minimum on both original legs, computes these intersections as well.
  - Explanation: Fibers of a compatible label on a pushout are the pushouts of the fibers, and objects are computed independently of labels.
- **claim-attachment-covers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-attachment-covers)): With the notation of Proposition <a href="#prop:labelled-pushout" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:labelled-pushout">12</a>, the following are canonical identifications of subcategories of $Y$.* 1. *Suppose $P_1,P_2$ are sieves of $P$, contain $A$, and cover $P$. Then the subcategories $$Y_i=X\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{P_i}\quad(i=1,2)$$ are sieves covering $Y$ on objects, and $$Y_1\cap Y_2 =X\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{P_1\cap P_2}.$$* 2. *Suppose $T_1,T_2$ are cosieves of $P$, cover $P$, satisfy $A\subset T_1$, and satisfy $A\cap T_2=\varnothing$. Then $$Y_1=X\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{T_1},\qquad Y_2=U_{T_2}$$ are cosieves covering $Y$ on objects, and $Y_1\cap Y_2=U_{T_1\cap T_2}$.* *If $A$ itself is a sieve of $P$, the canonical map $X\to Y$ is the inclusion of a full sieve.
  - Explanation: Sieve and cosieve covers of the attached poset induce covers of the pushout with controlled intersections.
- **claim-nerve-two-piece-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-nerve-cover)): Let $C_1,C_2\subset C$ be two full sieves which cover the objects of $C$, or two full cosieves which cover its objects. Put $C_{12}=C_1\cap C_2$. The square $$\begin{tikzcd} N_nC_{12} \ar[r,hook] \ar[d,hook] & N_nC_2 \ar[d,hook] \\ N_nC_1 \ar[r,hook] & N_nC \end{tikzcd}$$ is an ordinary pushout of simplicial sets. All four maps are monomorphisms, so it is also a homotopy pushout in the Kan–Quillen model structure.
  - Explanation: Street nerves turn two-piece full sieve or cosieve covers into pushouts of simplicial sets along monomorphisms.
- **claim-greatest-new-element** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-greatest-new)): Suppose that $A\hookrightarrow E$ is a sieve with a right adjoint retraction, and that $E\setminus A$ has a greatest element. For every attaching functor in <a href="#eq:couniversal-pushout" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:couniversal-pushout">[eq:couniversal-pushout]</a>, the map $j$ admits a retraction $p:Y\to X$ such that $N_n(jp)$ and $\mathrm{id}_{N_nY}$ are joined by a zigzag of simplicial homotopies. Consequently $j$ is a Thomason equivalence. Neither $A$ nor $E$ needs to be finite.
  - Explanation: When the new part has a greatest element, the inclusion into the pushout has a homotopy retraction, hence is a Thomason equivalence.
- **claim-couniversality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-couniversal)): If $i:A\hookrightarrow E$ is a sieve of small posets with a right adjoint retraction, then $c_nN(i)$ is a couniversal Thomason equivalence: for every $a:U_{A}\to X$, the canonical map $$X\longrightarrow X\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{E}$$ is a Thomason equivalence. This holds for every finite $n\ge1$ and for $n=\infty$.
  - Explanation: Poset sieve inclusions with a right adjoint retraction stay Thomason equivalences after every pushout, in every dimension.
- **claim-poset-unit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-poset-unit)): For every small poset $S$, the adjunction unit $$\eta_S:NS\longrightarrow N_nU_{S}$$ is a weak homotopy equivalence. This assertion is natural in $S$ and holds for every finite $n\ge1$ and for $n=\infty$.
  - Explanation: The adjunction unit on the nerve of any small poset is a weak homotopy equivalence.
- **claim-attachment-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-attachment-comparison)): Let $A\subset B$ be a sieve of small posets. Let $Z$ be its upward closure, and suppose that $A\hookrightarrow Z$ admits a right adjoint retraction. For every functor $a:U_{A}\to X$ in $n\mathrm{Cat}$, put $$V_B(X)=X\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{B},\qquad P_B(X)=N_nX\amalg_{N_nU_{A}}N_nU_{B}.$$ The canonical map $$\gamma_{B,X}:P_B(X)\longrightarrow N_nV_B(X) \label{eq:attachment-comparison}$$ is a simplicial weak equivalence. The map $N_nU_{A}\to N_nU_{B}$ is a monomorphism. The comparison is natural in every functor $f:X\to X'$ under $U_{A}$.
  - Explanation: Under the sieve and retraction hypotheses, the pushout of nerves compares by a weak equivalence to the nerve of the actual attachment.
- **claim-subdivision-posets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-subdivision-posets)): Let $d:K\hookrightarrow\Delta[m]$ be a boundary inclusion, with $m\geq0$, or a horn inclusion $\Lambda^k[m]\hookrightarrow\Delta[m]$, with $m\geq1$ and $0\leq k\leq m$. There are finite posets $A\subset B$ and a canonical identification $$\mathrm{Sd}^2 d=\bigl(NA\hookrightarrow NB\bigr).$$ Here $A$ is a sieve. Its upward closure $Z$ in $B$ is a cosieve, and $A\hookrightarrow Z$ has a right adjoint retraction.
  - Explanation: Twice-subdivided boundary and horn inclusions are nerve inclusions of finite poset pairs of the required sieve-with-retraction type.
- **claim-horn-attachments-acyclic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-horn-acyclic)): For every horn inclusion $d\in J$, every pushout of $L_n d$ belongs to $W_n$.
  - Explanation: Every pushout of a transferred horn inclusion is a weak equivalence of the model structure.
- **claim-attachment-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-attachment-invariance)): Let $d\in I\cup J$, write $L_n d$ as $U_{A}\to U_{B}$ using Lemma <a href="#lem:subdivision-posets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:subdivision-posets">19</a>, and let $f:X\to X'$ belong to $W_n$. For every $a:U_{A}\to X$, the induced functor $$X\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{B} \longrightarrow X'\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{B},$$ where the second attaching functor is $fa$, belongs to $W_n$.
  - Explanation: Attaching a transferred boundary or horn preserves weak equivalences between the attaching targets.
- **claim-transfinite-horns** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transfinite-horns)): Every relative $L_nJ$-cell map belongs to $W_n$.
  - Explanation: Transfinite composites of horn attachments remain weak equivalences by induction and filtered-colimit closure.
- **claim-transferred-model-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-model-structure)): The classes $(C_n,W_n,F_n)$ in <a href="#eq:model-classes" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:model-classes">[eq:model-classes]</a> form a combinatorial model structure on $n\mathrm{Cat}$, cofibrantly generated by $(L_nI,L_nJ)$.
  - Explanation: The prescribed classes form a combinatorial model structure generated by the transferred boundaries and horns, via the small object argument.
- **claim-properness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-properness)): The model structure of Proposition <a href="#prop:model-structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:model-structure">24</a> is left and right proper.
  - Explanation: The transferred model structure is left and right proper.
- **claim-subdivided-unit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-subdivided-unit)): For every simplicial set $K$, the canonical map $$\label{eq:subdivided-unit} \theta_K=\eta_{\mathrm{Sd}^2K}:\mathrm{Sd}^2K\longrightarrow N_nL_nK$$ is a weak equivalence.
  - Explanation: After two subdivisions the unit comparison is a weak equivalence for every simplicial set, by induction along boundary attachments.
- **claim-quillen-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-quillen-equivalence)): The adjunction $$L_n:\mathrm{sSet}_{\mathrm{KQ}}\rightleftarrows (n\mathrm{Cat},C_n,W_n,F_n):R_n$$ is a Quillen equivalence.
  - Explanation: The subdivided categorification and the twice-extended nerve form a Quillen equivalence with simplicial sets.
- **claim-strict-presentations** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-strict-presentations)): For every sorted set $G$ and every set $R$ of strong equations, there is a small strict $n$-category $P(G,R)$ such that, naturally in $Y$, strict functors $P(G,R)\to Y$ correspond to sorted assignments of the generators to cells of $Y$ satisfying $R$.
  - Explanation: Generators and strong equations present a small strict n-category, built by generating definedness judgments and equalities simultaneously.
- **claim-strict-presentability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-strict-presentability)): For every $1\le n\le\infty$, the category $n\mathrm{Cat}$ is locally small, complete, cocomplete, and locally finitely presentable. Filtered colimits are computed on cells. Every set of objects has a common infinite regular presentability bound.
  - Explanation: The category of strict n-categories is locally small, bicomplete and locally finitely presentable with cellwise filtered colimits and a common presentability bound.

      "explanation": "A cylinder stationary on the attaching source glues along any pushout of n-categories to a cylinder stationary on the target.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-poset-pieces",
        "exact_quote": "There is a canonical identification $\\operatorname{Ob}U_{P}=P$ for every poset $P$. If $S\\subset P$ is a sieve, the map $U_{S}\\to U_{P}$ is the inclusion of the full $0$-fiber of the membership label $U_{P}\\to D_{1}$. If $S$ is a cosieve, it is the inclusion of the full $1$-fiber. These identifications are natural for maps of labelled posets and include empty posets and empty fibers.",
        "source_id": "PM-317.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-poset-pieces",
          "exact_quote": "There is a canonical identification $\\operatorname{Ob}U_{P}=P$ for every poset $P$. If $S$ is a cosieve, it is the inclusion of the full $1$-fiber. These identifications are natural for maps of labelled posets and include empty posets and empty fibers.",
          "source_id": "PM-317.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a canonical identification $\\operatorname{Ob}U_{P}=P$ for every poset $P$. If $S$ is a cosieve, it is the inclusion of the full $1$-fiber. These identifications are natural for maps of labelled posets and include empty posets and empty fibers.",
      "explanation": "Objects of the categorified poset are the poset, and sieves and cosieves are full fibers of the membership label.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-labelled-pushout",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subset P$ be a full subposet, let $a:U_{A}\\to X$ be any strict functor, and form $$Y=X\\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{P}.$$ Suppose a monotone map $\\lambda:P\\to[1]$ and a strict functor $\\xi:X\\to D_{1}$ satisfy $$\\xi a=U(\\lambda|_A):U_{A}\\longrightarrow D_{1},\n \\qquad U_{[1]}=D_{1}.$$ Write $P_\\epsilon=\\lambda^{-1}(\\epsilon)$, $A_\\epsilon=A\\cap P_\\epsilon$, and $X_\\epsilon=\\xi^{-1}(\\epsilon)$. Then the induced label $Y\\to D_{1}$ has actual full fibers $$\\label{eq:pushout-fiber}\n Y_\\epsilon\\cong\n X_\\epsilon\\amalg_{U_{A_\\epsilon}}U_{P_\\epsilon}.$$ Moreover, independently of these labels, $$\\label{eq:pushout-objects}\n \\operatorname{Ob}Y\\cong\n \\operatorname{Ob}X\\amalg_A P\n \\cong\\operatorname{Ob}X\\amalg(P\\setminus A).$$ For two such pairs of compatible labels, write $\\bar\\lambda_1,\\bar\\lambda_2:Y\\to D_{1}$ for the induced labels. Their $0$-fibers intersect in the $0$-fiber of $\\max\\circ(\\bar\\lambda_1,\\bar\\lambda_2)$; their $1$-fibers intersect in the $1$-fiber of $\\min\\circ(\\bar\\lambda_1,\\bar\\lambda_2)$. Formula <a href=\"#eq:pushout-fiber\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pushout-fiber\">[eq:pushout-fiber]</a>, using the corresponding maximum or minimum on both original legs, computes these intersections as well.",
        "source_id": "PM-317.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-labelled-pushout",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subset P$ be a full subposet, let $a:U_{A}\\to X$ be any strict functor, and form $$Y=X\\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{P}.$$ Suppose a monotone map $\\lambda:P\\to[1]$ and a strict functor $\\xi:X\\to D_{1}$ satisfy $$\\xi a=U(\\lambda|_A):U_{A}\\longrightarrow D_{1},\n \\qquad U_{[1]}=D_{1}.$$ Write $P_\\epsilon=\\lambda^{-1}(\\epsilon)$, $A_\\epsilon=A\\cap P_\\epsilon$, and $X_\\epsilon=\\xi^{-1}(\\epsilon)$. Then the induced label $Y\\to D_{1}$ has actual full fibers $$\\label{eq:pushout-fiber}\n Y_\\epsilon\\cong\n X_\\epsilon\\amalg_{U_{A_\\epsilon}}U_{P_\\epsilon}.$$ Moreover, independently of these labels, $$\\label{eq:pushout-objects}\n \\operatorname{Ob}Y\\cong\n \\operatorname{Ob}X\\amalg_A P\n \\cong\\operatorname{Ob}X\\amalg(P\\setminus A).$$ For two such pairs of compatible labels, write $\\bar\\lambda_1,\\bar\\lambda_2:Y\\to D_{1}$ for the induced labels. Their $0$-fibers intersect in the $0$-fiber of $\\max\\circ(\\bar\\lambda_1,\\bar\\lambda_2)$; their $1$-fibers intersect in the $1$-fiber of $\\min\\circ(\\bar\\lambda_1,\\bar\\lambda_2)$. Formula <a href=\"#eq:pushout-fiber\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pushout-fiber\">[eq:pushout-fiber]</a>, using the corresponding maximum or minimum on both original legs, computes these intersections as well.",

        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $A\\subset P$ be a full subposet, let $a:U_{A}\\to X$ be any strict functor, and form $$Y=X\\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{P}.$$ Suppose a monotone map $\\lambda:P\\to[1]$ and a strict functor $\\xi:X\\to D_{1}$ satisfy $$\\xi a=U(\\lambda|_A):U_{A}\\longrightarrow D_{1},\n \\qquad U_{[1]}=D_{1}.$$ Write $P_\\epsilon=\\lambda^{-1}(\\epsilon)$, $A_\\epsilon=A\\cap P_\\epsilon$, and $X_\\epsilon=\\xi^{-1}(\\epsilon)$. Then the induced label $Y\\to D_{1}$ has actual full fibers $$\\label{eq:pushout-fiber}\n Y_\\epsilon\\cong\n X_\\epsilon\\amalg_{U_{A_\\epsilon}}U_{P_\\epsilon}.$$ Moreover, independently of these labels, $$\\label{eq:pushout-objects}\n \\operatorname{Ob}Y\\cong\n \\operatorname{Ob}X\\amalg_A P\n \\cong\\operatorname{Ob}X\\amalg(P\\setminus A).$$ For two such pairs of compatible labels, write $\\bar\\lambda_1,\\bar\\lambda_2:Y\\to D_{1}$ for the induced labels. Their $0$-fibers intersect in the $0$-fiber of $\\max\\circ(\\bar\\lambda_1,\\bar\\lambda_2)$; their $1$-fibers intersect in the $1$-fiber of $\\min\\circ(\\bar\\lambda_1,\\bar\\lambda_2)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-attachment-covers",
        "exact_quote": "With the notation of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:labelled-pushout\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:labelled-pushout\">12</a>, the following are canonical identifications of subcategories of $Y$.*\n\n1.  *Suppose $P_1,P_2$ are sieves of $P$, contain $A$, and cover $P$. Then the subcategories $$Y_i=X\\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{P_i}\\quad(i=1,2)$$ are sieves covering $Y$ on objects, and $$Y_1\\cap Y_2\n     =X\\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{P_1\\cap P_2}.$$*\n\n2.  *Suppose $T_1,T_2$ are cosieves of $P$, cover $P$, satisfy $A\\subset T_1$, and satisfy $A\\cap T_2=\\varnothing$. Then $$Y_1=X\\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{T_1},\\qquad Y_2=U_{T_2}$$ are cosieves covering $Y$ on objects, and $Y_1\\cap Y_2=U_{T_1\\cap T_2}$.*\n\n*If $A$ itself is a sieve of $P$, the canonical map $X\\to Y$ is the inclusion of a full sieve.",
        "source_id": "PM-317.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-attachment-covers",
          "exact_quote": "With the notation of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:labelled-pushout\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:labelled-pushout\">12</a>, the following are canonical identifications of subcategories of $Y$.*\n\n1.  *Suppose $P_1,P_2$ are sieves of $P$, contain $A$, and cover $P$. Then the subcategories $$Y_i=X\\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{P_i}\\quad(i=1,2)$$ are sieves covering $Y$ on objects, and $$Y_1\\cap Y_2\n     =X\\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{P_1\\cap P_2}.$$*\n\n2.  *Suppose $T_1,T_2$ are cosieves of $P$, cover $P$, satisfy $A\\subset T_1$, and satisfy $A\\cap T_2=\\varnothing$. Then $$Y_1=X\\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{T_1},\\qquad Y_2=U_{T_2}$$ are cosieves covering $Y$ on objects, and $Y_1\\cap Y_2=U_{T_1\\cap T_2}$.*\n\n*If $A$ itself is a sieve of $P$, the canonical map $X\\to Y$ is the inclusion of a full sieve.",
          "source_id": "PM-317.1"
        }

Then $$Y_1=X\\amalg_{U_{A}}U_{T_1},\\qquad Y_2=U_{T_2}$$ are cosieves covering $Y$ on objects, and $Y_1\\cap Y_2=U_{T_1\\cap T_2}$.*\n\n*If $A$ itself is a sieve of $P$, the canonical map $X\\to Y$ is the inclusion of a full sieve.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-nerve-cover",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C_1,C_2\\subset C$ be two full sieves which cover the objects of $C$, or two full cosieves which cover its objects. Put $C_{12}=C_1\\cap C_2$. The square $$\\begin{tikzcd}\n N_nC_{12} \\ar[r,hook] \\ar[d,hook] & N_nC_2 \\ar[d,hook] \\\\\n N_nC_1 \\ar[r,hook] & N_nC\n\\end{tikzcd}$$ is an ordinary pushout of simplicial sets. All four maps are monomorphisms, so it is also a homotopy pushout in the Kan–Quillen model structure.",
        "source_id": "PM-317.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

All four maps are monomorphisms, so it is also a homotopy pushout in the Kan–Quillen model structure.",
          "source_id": "PM-317.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-greatest-new",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $A\\hookrightarrow E$ is a sieve with a right adjoint retraction, and that $E\\setminus A$ has a greatest element. For every attaching functor in <a href=\"#eq:couniversal-pushout\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:couniversal-pushout\">[eq:couniversal-pushout]</a>, the map $j$ admits a retraction $p:Y\\to X$ such that $N_n(jp)$ and $\\mathrm{id}_{N_nY}$ are joined by a zigzag of simplicial homotopies. Consequently $j$ is a Thomason equivalence. Neither $A$ nor $E$ needs to be finite.",
        "source_id": "PM-317.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Consequently $j$ is a Thomason equivalence. Neither $A$ nor $E$ needs to be finite.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **left lifting class** ([source](#eq:prescribed-classes)): Here ${}^{\perp}$ denotes the left lifting class.
- **transferred adjoint pair** ([source](#eq:prescribed-classes)): With the usual subdivision adjunction $\mathrm{Sd}\dashv\mathrm{Ex}$, put $$L_n=c_n\mathrm{Sd}^2,\qquad R_n=\mathrm{Ex}^2N_n.$$
- **strong Steiner complex** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A complex with such a basis is called a *strong Steiner complex*.
- **poset sieve** ([source](#eq:poset-adjunction)): The inclusion is a *sieve* when $e\le a\in A$ implies $e\in A$.
- **Thomason equivalence** ([source](#sec:couniversality)): A *Thomason equivalence* is a strict functor whose Street nerve is a weak homotopy equivalence.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-internal-hom** [claim](#stmt-lem-internal-truncation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-internal-truncation): Compare a cylinder with its collapse onto the source face on atoms, then transpose through the reflection. -> internal hom is an n-category
- **step-gray-nerve** [claim](#stmt-lem-gray-nerve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gray-nerve): Alexander-Whitney diagonal as a positive chain map, transported by Steiner's functor. -> simplicial homotopy from a cylinder
- **step-filtered** [claim](#stmt-lem-filtered) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-filtered): Essentially algebraic finitary theory, cellwise filtered colimits, Kan replacement. -> filtered colimit closure
- **step-simplicial-gluing** [claim](#stmt-lem-simplicial-gluing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-simplicial-gluing): Left properness and a left Quillen cobase change on undercategories. -> gluing with one monomorphic leg
- **step-prism** [claim](#stmt-lem-relative-prism) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-relative-prism): Simplexwise positive chain map, naturality in simplex operators, assembly by colimit and reflection. -> stationary cylinder on posets
- **step-pushout-cylinder** [claim](#stmt-lem-pushout-cylinder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pushout-cylinder): Transpose cylinders to maps into the internal hom and use the pushout universal property. -> cylinder on the pushout
- **step-fibers** [claim](#stmt-lem-fibers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fibers): Explicit right adjoints to fiber functors and a chaotic category for objects. -> colimit preservation of fibers and objects
- **step-oriental-segments** [claim](#stmt-lem-oriental-segments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-oriental-segments): Support of expressions in atoms plus a monotone clamping retraction. -> segments are full suborientals
- **step-poset-pieces** [claim](#stmt-lem-poset-pieces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-poset-pieces): Simplex colimit presentation, fibers commute with colimits. -> sieves are full fibers
- **step-labelled-pushout** [claim](#stmt-prop-labelled-pushout) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-labelled-pushout): Pushout in the slice over the one-cell category, then apply fiber functors. -> fiber formula for attachments
- **step-attachment-covers** [claim](#stmt-cor-attachment-covers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-attachment-covers): Apply the labelled attachment formula to chosen labels and their max or min. -> covers of the pushout
- **step-nerve-cover** [claim](#stmt-lem-nerve-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nerve-cover): Every simplex lies in one piece; union of simplicial subsets is a pushout. -> nerve pushout square
- **step-greatest-new** [claim](#stmt-lem-greatest-new) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-greatest-new): Two monotone maps below a common constant-on-new upper map, glued cylinders and a retraction. -> homotopy retraction
- **step-couniversal** [claim](#stmt-thm-couniversal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-couniversal): Induction on new elements by deleting maximal ones, then filtered union of retraction-stable finite subposets. -> couniversal Thomason equivalence
- **step-poset-unit** [claim](#stmt-prop-poset-unit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-poset-unit): Induction on cardinality using a contraction when a greatest element exists, sieve cover otherwise, then filtered colimit. -> unit is a weak equivalence on posets
- **step-attachment-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-attachment-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-attachment-comparison): Compare over the upward closure by couniversality, then glue a cosieve cover with left properness. -> comparison weak equivalence
- **step-subdivision-posets** [claim](#stmt-lem-subdivision-posets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-subdivision-posets): Chains of faces describe the double subdivision; intersection with the retained faces is the retraction. -> poset pair with right adjoint retraction
- **step-horn-acyclic** [claim](#stmt-cor-horn-acyclic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-horn-acyclic): Poset unit and two-out-of-three give a trivial cofibration; compose with the comparison. -> horn pushouts are weak equivalences
- **step-attachment-invariance** [claim](#stmt-cor-attachment-invariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-attachment-invariance): Left properness of simplicial sets and two-out-of-three on the naturality square. -> invariance under attachment
- **step-transfinite-horns** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfinite-horns) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfinite-horns): Transfinite induction with filtered colimit closure at limit stages. -> relative cell maps are weak equivalences
- **step-model-structure** [claim](#stmt-prop-model-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-model-structure): Small object argument for both generating sets and identification of lifting classes by adjunction. -> combinatorial model structure
- **step-properness** [claim](#stmt-prop-properness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-properness): Right properness through the right adjoint; left properness by induction over boundary attachments and retracts. -> left and right proper
- **step-subdivided-unit** [claim](#stmt-prop-subdivided-unit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-subdivided-unit): Cell induction on simplicial sets using gluing and the attachment comparison. -> unit after two subdivisions
- **step-quillen-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-thm-quillen-equivalence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-quillen-equivalence): Compute the unit of the composite adjunction and check the equivalence criterion. -> Quillen equivalence
- **step-strict-presentations** [claim](#stmt-lem-strict-presentations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-strict-presentations): Least closed set of definedness and equality judgments, quotient by derivable equality. -> presented strict category
- **step-presentability** [claim](#stmt-prop-strict-presentability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-strict-presentability): Colimits by presentation, limits coordinatewise, filtered colimits cellwise, finite presentations. -> local presentability
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#stmt-thm-main): Combine the model structure, properness and Quillen equivalence. -> main theorem

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-sieve-of-chains**: In the finite construction of the subdivision posets, the poset A of chains lying inside the face poset of K is a sieve of B: every nonempty subchain of a chain of A is again in A.
- **inv-right-adjoint-retraction**: Intersecting a chain of the upward closure Z with the face poset of K gives a monotone retraction onto A that is right adjoint to the inclusion of A into Z.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-non-boundary-horn-inputs**: Applying the poset description to an arbitrary simplicial inclusion rather than a boundary or horn inclusion. -> The description of the face poset of K is only derived for boundaries and horns, so the retraction and sieve properties are not guaranteed elsewhere.
- **anti-directed-semantics**: Reading the weak equivalences obtained from these poset pieces as preserving directed or irreversible structure. -> The weak equivalences are detected through the Street nerve and the paper imposes no invertibility on positive-dimensional cells, so they identify objects only up to homotopy type.
- **anti-sieve-without-retraction**: Treating any sieve inclusion of posets as couniversal after pushout. -> The couniversality theorem needs the right adjoint retraction; a bare sieve gives no contraction.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Lemma 19 gives an explicit finite construction of the posets A, B and Z and of the retraction for each boundary or horn inclusion, so small cases can be built and the sieve and adjunction properties checked exhaustively. The main model-structure results are non-constructive and are not covered.

```python
# INPUTS: one line 'kind m k' with kind boundary or horn, integer m (m >= 1 for horn) and integer k with 0 <= k <= m (k ignored for boundary)
# PRECONDITIONS: small m (at most 3) so that all chains of nonempty subsets can be enumerated
# OUTPUTS: one line 'B=<n> A=<n> Z=<n> sieve=<0|1> adj=<0|1>' with the chain counts and the two property checks
# TIME: exponential in m, enumerates every nonempty chain of nonempty subsets of {0..m}
# SPACE: proportional to the number of chains
# ARITHMETIC: exact integers and finite sets, no floating point
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and exits nonzero
import sys
from itertools import combinations

def main():
    kind, m, k = sys.stdin.read().split()
    m = int(m)
    k = int(k)
    M = frozenset(range(m + 1))
    P = [frozenset(c) for s in range(1, m + 2) for c in combinations(range(m + 1), s)]
    if kind == "boundary":
        PK = {F for F in P if F != M}
    else:
        PK = {F for F in P if not (M - {k}) <= F}
    chains = []
    def grow(ch):
        chains.append(tuple(ch))
        for F in P:
            if ch[-1] < F:
                grow(ch + [F])
    for F in P:
        grow([F])
    Bs = [frozenset(c) for c in chains]
    As = [c for c in Bs if c <= PK]
    Zs = [c for c in Bs if c & PK]
    Aset = set(As)
    sieve = all(b in Aset for a in As for b in Bs if b <= a)
    adj = 1
    for s in Zs:
        r = s & PK
        if r not in Aset:
            adj = 0
        for al in As:
            if (al <= s) != (al <= r):
                adj = 0
    print("B=%d A=%d Z=%d sieve=%d adj=%d" % (len(Bs), len(As), len(Zs), int(sieve), adj))

main()
```

| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a theorem about classes of maps (cofibrations, weak equivalences, fibrations). It gives no checker, converter or other tool with defined inputs and outputs. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The factorizations come from a transfinite small object argument over infinite regular cardinals. No finite procedure, termination argument or cost bound is given. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper presents strict categories by generators and strong equations, but only to prove local presentability. Composites created by identifications can make presentations infinite, so no finite representation is supplied. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No arithmetic kernel, memory layout or parallel scheme appears. The content is purely categorical and homotopical. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, adversary model or protocol appears anywhere in the paper. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no data, loss or learning problem. The Quillen equivalence compares homotopy theories and does not model statistical learning. |

| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Weak equivalences here ignore direction and ordering of cells. Treating them as equivalences of dependency or execution structures would erase causal order. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-retraction-required** (hypothesis): Couniversality is proved only for a sieve of small posets whose inclusion has a right adjoint retraction. A bare sieve inclusion is not covered, and the proof uses the retraction to build the contraction. Blocked use: ['Invoking couniversality for an arbitrary sieve inclusion of posets without checking that a right adjoint retraction exists', 'Concluding that a pushout along a sieve without retraction is a Thomason equivalence']
- **lim-prism-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The relative prism is built only for a downward-closed subposet with two monotone maps that are pointwise ordered, agree on the old part and are constant on the new part. The chain formula is positive only under these hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Applying the prism formula to monotone maps that are not pointwise comparable', 'Applying the prism formula when the upper map is not constant off the old subposet']
- **lim-no-invertibility** (formal_scope): The weak equivalences are detected through the Street nerve and no invertibility is assumed on positive-dimensional cells. They therefore encode homotopy types of nerves, not any directed or order-sensitive notion of equivalence. Blocked use: ['Reading weak equivalences of this model structure as preserving irreversible or causal order', 'Using the equivalence to identify directed structures that differ in direction']
- **lim-nonconstructive-factorisation** (nonconstructive): The factorizations are obtained by a transfinite small object argument over a locally presentable category, using a regular cardinal bound whose existence is only asserted. No finite procedure or cost bound is given. Blocked use: ['Quoting a running time or termination guarantee for computing fibrant or cofibrant replacements', 'Implementing the factorization as a finite algorithm on presented categories']
- **lim-finite-dimension-truncation** (hypothesis): The statement that the internal hom is again an n-category is for finite n and a strict n-category target. The tensor with the interval is taken in the infinite-dimensional setting and can have cells above n, so cylinders must be transposed rather than treated as n-categories. Blocked use: ['Treating the tensor with the interval as an object of the n-dimensional category', 'Applying the truncation statement to a target that is not a strict n-category']
- **lim-boundary-horn-only** (edge_case): The explicit poset description of the double subdivision is derived only for boundary inclusions and horn inclusions with the stated index ranges. Other simplicial inclusions are not covered by the sieve and retraction conclusion. Blocked use: ['Applying the poset pair and retraction to an arbitrary monomorphism of simplicial sets', 'Using horn indices or simplex dimensions outside the stated ranges']
- **lim-presentations-may-be-infinite** (edge_case): A presentation of a strict category must generate defined composites together with equations. Identifying boundaries can create new composable pairs, so a presented category need not have finitely many cells even from finite data. Blocked use: ['Representing a presented strict category as a finite table of cells and assuming closure under composites', 'Assuming that a finite presentation yields a finite category']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 317 (`lean/docs/317.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Thomason Model Structures in Every Strict Higher Dimension](../../preprints/Thomason-Model-Structures-in-Every-Strict-Higher-Dimension-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves the higher-dimensional Thomason model-structure theorem for small strict globular $n$-categories in every positive finite dimension and in dimension $\omega$. Each category has the stated proper combinatorial model structure, Quillen equivalent to simplicial sets through the twice-subdivided categorification and twice-extended Street nerve adjunction.
>
> The selected statements also identify weak equivalences by the Street nerve in all these dimensions. Thus the constructions model the homotopy theory of spaces throughout the stated range.

```lean
structure OmegaCategory where
  Cell : Type u
  source : ℕ → Cell → Cell
  target : ℕ → Cell → Cell
  source_source (i j : ℕ) (x : Cell) : source i (source j x) = source (min i j) x
  target_target (i j : ℕ) (x : Cell) : target i (target j x) = target (min i j) x
  source_target (i j : ℕ) (x : Cell) :
    source i (target j x) = if i < j then source i x else target j x
  target_source (i j : ℕ) (x : Cell) :
    target i (source j x) = if i < j then target i x else source j x
  comp (k : ℕ) (x y : Cell) : target k x = source k y → Cell
  source_comp_le (i k : ℕ) (hik : i ≤ k) (x y : Cell)
      (h : target k x = source k y) : source i (comp k x y h) = source i x
  target_comp_le (i k : ℕ) (hik : i ≤ k) (x y : Cell)
      (h : target k x = source k y) : target i (comp k x y h) = target i y
  source_comp_gt (i k : ℕ) (hki : k < i) (x y : Cell)
      (h : target k x = source k y)
      (hs : target k (source i x) = source k (source i y)) :
    source i (comp k x y h) = comp k (source i x) (source i y) hs
  target_comp_gt (i k : ℕ) (hki : k < i) (x y : Cell)
      (h : target k x = source k y)
      (ht : target k (target i x) = source k (target i y)) :
    target i (comp k x y h) = comp k (target i x) (target i y) ht
  unit_left (k : ℕ) (x : Cell) (h : target k (source k x) = source k x) :
    comp k (source k x) x h = x
  unit_right (k : ℕ) (x : Cell) (h : target k x = source k (target k x)) :
    comp k x (target k x) h = x
  assoc (k : ℕ) (x y z : Cell)
      (hxy : target k x = source k y) (hyz : target k y = source k z)
      (hxy_z : target k (comp k x y hxy) = source k z)
      (hx_yz : target k x = source k (comp k y z hyz)) :
    comp k (comp k x y hxy) z hxy_z = comp k x (comp k y z hyz) hx_yz
  interchange (k l : ℕ) (hkl : k < l) (a b c d : Cell)
      (hab : target l a = source l b) (hcd : target l c = source l d)
      (hac : target k a = source k c) (hbd : target k b = source k d)
      (hab_cd : target k (comp l a b hab) = source k (comp l c d hcd))
      (hac_bd : target l (comp k a c hac) = source l (comp k b d hbd)) :
    comp k (comp l a b hab) (comp l c d hcd) hab_cd =
      comp l (comp k a c hac) (comp k b d hbd) hac_bd
  finite_dimension (x : Cell) : ∃ k, source k x = x
```

```lean
structure Functor (C : OAI.Thomason.OmegaCategory.{u}) (D : OAI.Thomason.OmegaCategory.{v}) where
  map : C.Cell → D.Cell
  map_source (k : ℕ) (x : C.Cell) : map (C.source k x) = D.source k (map x)
  map_target (k : ℕ) (x : C.Cell) : map (C.target k x) = D.target k (map x)
  map_comp (k : ℕ) (x y : C.Cell) (h : C.target k x = C.source k y)
      (h' : D.target k (map x) = D.source k (map y)) :
    map (C.comp k x y h) = D.comp k (map x) (map y) h'
```

```lean
structure DirectedComplex where
  Chain : ℕ → Type u
  group : ∀ p, AddCommGroup (Chain p)
  d : (p : ℕ) → Chain (p+1) →+ Chain p
  d_d (p : ℕ) (x : Chain (p+2)) : d p (d (p+1) x) = 0
  augmentation : Chain 0 →+ ℤ
  augmentation_d (x : Chain 1) : augmentation (d 0 x) = 0
  positive : (p : ℕ) → AddSubmonoid (Chain p)
```

```lean
structure Hom (K : OAI.Thomason.DirectedComplex.{u}) (L : OAI.Thomason.DirectedComplex.{v}) where
  map (p : ℕ) : K.Chain p →+ L.Chain p
  map_d (p : ℕ) (x : K.Chain (p+1)) : map p (K.d p x) = L.d p (map (p+1) x)
  map_augmentation (x : K.Chain 0) : L.augmentation (map 0 x) = K.augmentation x
  map_positive (p : ℕ) (x : K.Chain p) : x ∈ K.positive p → map p x ∈ L.positive p
```

```lean
structure Table (K : OAI.Thomason.DirectedComplex.{u}) where
  row : (p : ℕ) → Bool → K.Chain p
  positive (p : ℕ) (b : Bool) : row p b ∈ K.positive p
  boundary (p : ℕ) (b : Bool) :
    K.d p (row (p+1) b) = row p true - row p false
  augmentation (b : Bool) : K.augmentation (row 0 b) = 1
  bounded : ∃ k, ∀ p, k < p → ∀ b, row p b = 0
```

```lean
structure ExactModelEndpoint {C : Type v} [Category.{u} C]
    (L : SSet.{u} ⥤ C) (R : C ⥤ SSet.{u}) (adj : L ⊣ R) where
  model : ModelCategory C
  weak_eq : model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences =
    OAI.Thomason.simplicialWeakEquivalences.inverseImage R
  fib_eq : model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations = OAI.Thomason.simplicialFibrations.inverseImage R
  cof_eq : model.categoryWithCofibrations.cofibrations =
    ((OAI.Thomason.simplicialFibrations.inverseImage R) ⊓ (OAI.Thomason.simplicialWeakEquivalences.inverseImage R)).llp
  generating_cofibrations : (OAI.Thomason.boundaryImages L).rlp =
    model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations ⊓ model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences
  generating_trivial_cofibrations : (OAI.Thomason.hornImages L).rlp = model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations
  weak_factorization_one : MorphismProperty.IsWeakFactorizationSystem
    (model.categoryWithCofibrations.cofibrations ⊓ model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences)
    model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations
  weak_factorization_two : MorphismProperty.IsWeakFactorizationSystem
    model.categoryWithCofibrations.cofibrations
    (model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations ⊓ model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences)
  complete : HasLimitsOfSize.{u,u} C
  cocomplete : HasColimitsOfSize.{u,u} C
  locallyPresentable : IsLocallyPresentable.{u} C
  leftProper : OAI.Thomason.LeftProperFor model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences
    model.categoryWithCofibrations.cofibrations
  rightProper : OAI.Thomason.RightProperFor model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences
    model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations
  leftQuillen : ∀ {K K' : SSet.{u}} (f : K ⟶ K'), Mono f →
    model.categoryWithCofibrations.cofibrations (L.map f) ∧
      (OAI.Thomason.simplicialWeakEquivalences f → model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences (L.map f))
  quillenEquivalence : ∀ (K : SSet.{u}) (Y : C),
    OAI.Thomason.FibrantFor model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations Y →
    ∀ f : L.obj K ⟶ Y,
      model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences f ↔
        OAI.Thomason.simplicialWeakEquivalences (adj.homEquiv K Y f)
```

```lean
theorem Thomason.nerveDetection_allDimensions :
    OAI.Thomason.thomasonWeakEquivalences.{u} =
      OAI.Thomason.simplicialWeakEquivalences.inverseImage OAI.Thomason.streetNerveFunctor ∧
    ∀ n : ℕ, OAI.Thomason.thomasonWeakEquivalencesFinite.{u} n =
      OAI.Thomason.simplicialWeakEquivalences.inverseImage (OAI.Thomason.streetNerveFinite n)

(OAI.Thomason.simplicialWeakEquivalences.inverseImage R)).llp\n  generating_cofibrations : (OAI.Thomason.boundaryImages L).rlp =\n    model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations ⊓ model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences\n  generating_trivial_cofibrations : (OAI.Thomason.hornImages L).rlp = model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations\n  weak_factorization_one : MorphismProperty.IsWeakFactorizationSystem\n    (model.categoryWithCofibrations.cofibrations ⊓ model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences)\n    model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations\n  weak_factorization_two : MorphismProperty.IsWeakFactorizationSystem\n    model.categoryWithCofibrations.cofibrations\n    (model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations ⊓ model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences)\n  complete : HasLimitsOfSize.{u,u} C\n  cocomplete : HasColimitsOfSize.{u,u} C\n  locallyPresentable : IsLocallyPresentable.{u} C\n  leftProper : OAI.Thomason.LeftProperFor model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences\n    model.categoryWithCofibrations.cofibrations\n  rightProper : OAI.Thomason.RightProperFor model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences\n    model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations\n  leftQuillen : ∀ {K K' : SSet.{u}} (f : K ⟶ K'), Mono f →\n    model.categoryWithCofibrations.cofibrations (L.map f) ∧\n      (OAI.Thomason.simplicialWeakEquivalences f → model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences (L.map f))\n  quillenEquivalence : ∀ (K : SSet.{u}) (Y : C),\n    OAI.Thomason.FibrantFor model.categoryWithFibrations.fibrations Y →\n    ∀ f : L.obj K ⟶ Y,\n      model.categoryWithWeakEquivalences.weakEquivalences f ↔\n        OAI.Thomason.simplicialWeakEquivalences (adj.homEquiv K Y f)"

    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Thomason Model Structures in Every Strict Higher Dimension](../../preprints/Thomason-Model-Structures-in-Every-Strict-Higher-Dimension-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe formalization proves the higher-dimensional Thomason model-structure theorem for small strict globular $n$-categories in every positive finite dimension and in dimension $\\omega$. Each category has the stated proper combinatorial model structure, Quillen equivalent to simplicial sets through the twice-subdivided categorification and twice-extended Street nerve adjunction.\n\nThe selected statements also identify weak equivalences by the Street nerve in all these dimensions. Thus the constructions model the homotopy theory of spaces throughout the stated range."

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the higher-dimensional Thomason model-structure conjecture of Ara and Maltsiniotis. For every $`1\le n\le\infty`$, the category of small strict globular <i>n</i>-categories admits a proper combinatorial model structure that is Quillen equivalent to simplicial sets. Its weak equivalences and fibrations are detected by the twice-extended Street nerve $`\mathrm{Ex}^2N_n`$, and the Quillen equivalence is given by $`c_n\mathrm{Sd}^2\dashv\mathrm{Ex}^2N_n`$. Thus strict higher categories model the homotopy theory of spaces in every positive finite dimension and in dimension <i>ω</i>.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
